January 25, 2024 10:22 PM GMT

Asia – Apparel and Footwear Supply Chain | Asia Pacific

# 2024 Outlook: On Front Foot for Multi-year Upcycle

We expect the normalization of order intakes and summer Olympics to drive OEMs' top-line strength to some extent in 2024, boosting capacity utilization and supporting margins. Supply consolidation and supply chain rebalancing should further drive a multi-year upcycle for quality OEMs, in our view.

2024 set to be a top-line growth story for apparel/footwear OEMs: With major global brands and retailers working down their inventory in 2023, we expect Asian OEMs to see a customer order recovery. We think 1H24 will still be a process of normalization of order placement instead of active restocking, as the overall economic outlook is still unclear. However, if the global economy is resilient without major setbacks, we think OEMs' order books will see sustained growth into 2H24. While the Summer Olympics could drive related product orders, we think it will act as more of a sentiment driver. Overall we are positive on the Asian apparel and footwear OEMs' outlook in 2024 and onwards given these near-term factors and structural supply-side consolidation that has accelerated since the pandemic, driven by demand shock in 2Q-3Q20, Vietnam lockdown in 3Q-4Q21, and end-market inventory destocking in 4Q22-3Q23. Also, ongoing supply chain rebalancing needs from brands/retailers should continue to favor large OEMs with resources to diversify their production bases, garnering more orders in the long run. All in, we expect apparel OEMs to register an average of 14% revenue growth in 2024, while footwear OEMs book 12% top-line growth on average.

Higher capacity utilization a general positive for OEMs' margins but FX could be a swing to margin trend: Given 2023's destocking cycle, most OEMs have not expanded capacity, setting a good backdrop for higher capacity utilization rates in 2024 to support GPM and hence the OPM expansion track on operating leverage. However, with some OEMs showing good 2023 margin expansion, we view current FX trends, i.e. Asian currency appreciation on capital inflow to emerging markets, as a major swing for OEMs' margin expansion. Overall, we forecast apparel OEMs to expand GPM and OPM by 66bps and 91bps each in 2024, while we expect footwear OEMs to expand GPM and OPM by 183bps and 242bps each in 2024.

Balanced stock selection between apparel and footwear OEMs: We expect apparel OEMs to see more apparent order recovery given more room for share gains and faster recovery of apparel brands/retailers. On the other hand, we think footwear OEMs' fundamentals will recover into 2024 but this is unlikely to be significant. Our top picks are Eclat, Shenzhou, and Crystal (upgraded to OW) for apparel OEMs, while we prefer Stella, Fulgent Sun, and Huali (upgraded to OW) for footwear OEMs. Our least preferred OEM is Feng Tay given rich valuation with lukewarm fundamentals, which we think will drive its gradual de-rating.

# 2024 Outlook: On Front Foot for Multi-year Earnings Upcycle

After the end-market inventory destocking cycle in 2023 due to brands/retailers' aggressive inventory build after supply chain disruptions in 2022 (productions and logistics), we think 2024 will be the start of a multi-year upcycle for major Asian apparel and footwear OEMs. The near-term driver comes mainly from the normalization of brands/retailers' order placement that should help most OEMs to grow their order books and hence revenue/earnings off a low base, especially 1H24. The structural driver comes mainly from ongoing supply-side consolidation (which has accelerated since the pandemic) and supply chain rebalancing in reaction to brands/retailers' need for diversified procurement to reduce risks. If the global economy remains steady without major setbacks, we are confident that 2024 will see major OEMs outperform in terms of share prices and see further stock re-rating. The 2024 summer Olympics could also offer positive sentiment for Asian apparel/footwear OEMs, albeit fundamental benefits could be limited.

However, we do not think the tide will lift all boats, as some OEMs may see more lukewarm revenue/earnings growth and some may have to deal with individual issues such as execution, customer mix change etc, on top of likely unattractive valuation. Therefore, even though we are positive on the sector's outlook, we are still selective in our stock picks.

Investment strategy - balanced positions between apparel and footwear OEMs; remain selective on stocks with apparent structural positives and/or attractive valuations: We expect apparel OEMs to see more apparent order recovery on the back of share gains and faster recovery of apparel brands/retailers. On the other hand, we see footwear OEMs fundamentals recovering into 2024 as they offer better margin elasticity given their laborintensive nature, hence driving better earnings growth.

Our key picks are Eclat and Shenzhou for apparel OEMs. We also upgrade Crystal to OW from UW as we think it will benefit from the order upcycle, along with market share gains, after multi-year adjustments on capacity allocation and product mix, on top of attractive valuation. We downgrade Makalot to EW from OW as near-term re-rating potential is largely in the price and downgrade Regina Miracle to EW from OW (given uncertain recovery strength from its major customers, in our view), while we keep Pacific Textiles at UW.

We prefer Stella (OW), Huali (upgraded to OW from EW on rare structural share gain story), and Fulgent Sun (OW) for footwear OEMs. We maintain our EW on Yue Yuen and UW on Taiwan Paiho. Our least preferred footwear OEM is Feng Tay (UW) given rich valuation and lukewarm fundamentals, which we think will drive a gradual de-rating of the stock.

## 2024 set to see OEM top-line growth

Asian apparel and footwear OEMs underwent a tough end-market destocking cycle for most of 2023 with order and revenue declines. However, this was driven more by inventory overbuild in 2022 and, to a certain extent, brands/retailers' cautious order placement given an uncertain economic outlook (e.g. concerns on US recession emerged in late 2022 and were an overhang for many companies in 2023). However, we have continued to see resilient end-demand for apparel and footwear in the US, which has capped the inventory-to-sales level in the market, setting a good foundation for Asian apparel and footwear OEMs' order outlook in 2024. We note the overall global economic outlook remains uncertain as we head into 2024 so we think the initial stage of the OEMs' order recovery (4Q23-2Q24) will come mainly from the normalization of order placement instead of strong restocking demand. If the global economy is stable and end-market sales for apparel/footwear are resilient, then we can become more confident on OEMs' 3Q-4Q24 outlook as restocking demand would then start to accelerate as well as growing off a low-base on Fall/Winter product inventory in 2023.

In addition to the possible recovery for Asian OEMs, structurally we are positive on their outlook given apparent supply-side consolidation and supply chain rebalancing, which will continue to favor larger OEMs that have resources and scale advantages to meet brands/ retailers' needs. We therefore believe 2024 will be the start of a multi-year order upcycle for Asia apparel and footwear OEMs, if there are no major negative events. All in, we expect apparel OEMs to register an average of 14% revenue growth in 2024 with footwear OEMs booking 12% top-line growth on average.

Demand and inventory appears to be healthy in the US; China market's outlook still uncertain

The market has been concerned about an end-demand slowdown in the US since late-2022. However, even though the brands and retailers have undergone an inventory destocking cycle since then, we have not seen demand soften. US retail inventories/sales ratio for apparel ( Exhibit 4 ) remains below pre-Covid levels, indicating that the destocking was due more to inventory overbuild instead of weak demand in the end market. Therefore, as major brands' and retailers' inventory growth has returned to more normalised levels ( Exhibit 6 and Exhibit 7 ), we believe this bodes well for Asian OEMs' order outlook into 2024 as the normalization of order placement from these customers can drive OEMs' revenue and earnings growth with limited downside risk now.

That said, the China market remains a key swing factor as the recovery pace and strength since the reopening in late-2022 has been somewhat slower and softer than we anticipated. Exhibit 8 to Exhibit 10 indicate that it appears that the demand for apparel/ footwear in China has bottomed in recent months, but it is still uncertain whether there will be improvement in end demand throughout 2024 to make OEMs better positioned in the Chinese market. We do think the market has priced in this swing though and any further improvement would offer upside risk to current market expectations.

## Supply-side consolidation still ongoing and to the benefit for large-size OEMs

Structurally, we believe supply-side consolidation and supply chain rebalancing will continue to favor large apparel and footwear OEMs. We have observed an acceleration of supply-side consolidation since 2020 due mainly to the pandemic. There were three major waves of such consolidation: 1) 2Q-3Q20: demand shock due to US and Europe lockdowns that saw many OEMs see almost no orders during that period; 2) 3Q21-4Q21 Vietnam lockdown: disrupting OEMs' production that drove out small- to medium OEMs with working capital issues; and 3) 4Q22-3Q23 inventory destocking cycle. We think the acceleration of supply-side consolidation has also been a driver behind large OEMs' order recovery since 2H23 when some of their customers were still digesting inventory and/or had uncertain growth outlooks. Such market share gains have helped these OEMs benefit more and faster amid the normalization of customers' order placements.

With new global trade dynamics and in view of supply chain disruptions during the pandemic, brands/retailers have increasing needs to diversify their procurement to reduce supply risks. This will continue to benefit large OEMs as they have more resources to diversify their production bases (or add more capacity in existing sites) and also have better support from large, or more, customers to fulfill their newly added capacity in customers' desired production locations.

Apparel OEMs: Faster and stronger recovery helped by earlier start of destocking cycle and more room for share gains

Apparel OEMs' customers, especially mass channel in the US, started destocking as early as late-2Q22 when they saw inventory overbuild. Sports apparel retailers also joined the destocking cycle from 3Q22. We believe apparel inventory will be digested faster given the earlier start of destocking and as apparel products are more seasonal, so brands/ retailers tend to use larger discounts to offload off-season products. We believe this bodes well for an earlier recovery for apparel OEMs' order intakes. In addition, the apparel industry has been fragmented in end-markets and the OEM field, i.e. there are many brands and many OEMs. Therefore, we think there is more room for apparel OEMs to obtain market share not only from the aforementioned three waves of supply-side consolidation but also the fact that, in the early stage of normalization of order placement, brands/retailers may consolidate their order placements to large suppliers given their ability to provide more flexibility in production lead times with quality finished goods, on top of a more simplified procurement process without dealing with multiple suppliers.

Despite the destocking cycle in 2023, we have continued to see resilient demand for functional apparel, evidenced by some major brands' solid business momentum, such as Lululemon. We believe this is positive for textile/apparel makers that specialize in the functional wear area. In addition, emerging brands have garnered consumers' attention in recent years but these brands' initial focus has been on footwear. Now we do see some of them putting increasing focus on apparel, such as Hoka and On Running. We think this bodes well for textile/apparel OEMs that have good innovation capability and have business strategies to continuously expand their customer bases, such as Eclat (in addition to Lululemon, Athleta, Under Armour, the OEMs also have exposure to Hoka).

Footwear OEMs: The nature of a more concentrated market dynamic for brands and OEMs may cap recovery strength

We think order placements to footwear OEMs may see the recovery timing later than those for apparel OEMs and the strength of the recovery may also not be as good as the market bulls' expectations. Footwear OEMs saw solid shipments until 3Q22, so that meant the destocking of footwear brands/retailers was later than that of apparel brands/ retailers. This is due to Vietnam's lockdown in 3Q-4Q22, when many footwear OEMs closed their factories while apparel OEMs in Vietnam were still able to run at 20-30% of capacity utilization (as they were less labor intensive, they could fulfill local regulations on operating factories with containment of worker traffic and employee hygiene amid the lockdowns). Footwear brands/retailers' overbuild on inventory in view of supply chain disruption thus lasted longer - as they provided longer order delivery times, when they realized the end-market inventory issues they could not pullback but had to honor their orders.

In addition, there is less seasonality in footwear products, especially for athleisure shoes. Hence, inventory destocking could also last longer. The footwear market is also more concentrated in both end markets and OEMs if we look more specifically at the sports and casual categories. The sports footwear category has been dominated by some big brands, while in OEMs' customer mix, we can also see more concentrated customer base compared to apparel OEMs. Therefore, this means footwear OEMs' have less room for market share gains amid a downcycle and they move in-line with their customers' business trends.

Against this backdrop, we think OEMs that offer a share gain story, focusing on niche categories, and/or are exposed to emerging brands with better growth potential can fare better than peers.

## OEMs' margins set to continue to expand on higher capacity utilization and stable raw material prices

Given the destocking in 2023 and uncertain outlook for brands/retailer customers (as they faced both high inventory and an uncertain global economy), most OEMs have not expanded or reduced their capacities (e.g. Yue Yuen has been optimizing its workforce since 1Q23), setting a good backdrop for higher capacity utilization rates in 2024 to support GPM and hence the OPM expansion track on operating leverage. However, some OEMs saw good 2023 margin expansion thanks to good utilization of their capacity, due to product mix upgrades and efficiency improvements. We view current FX trends, i.e. potential Asian currency appreciation on capital inflows to emerging markets, as a major swing factor for OEMs' margin expansion and earnings growth in 2024. Overall, we forecast apparel OEMs to expand GPM and OPM by 66bps and 91bps each in 2024, while we expect footwear OEMs to expand GPM and OPM by 183bps and 242bps each in 2024, resulting in apparel OEMs' average net earnings growth of 21% and footwear OEMs' average net earnings growth of 43% in 2024.

## FX trend to be the major swing

Our new earnings forecasts for the OEMs factor in a scenario and impact on earnings from appreciation of Asian currencies vs. USD (particularly TWD) driven by capital inflows to emerging markets. For 1H24, the major reporting currency for apparel/footwear OEMs under our coverage - i.e. TWD and RMB, are likely to continue to depreciate on a YoY basis, so the margin impact could be limited. We do think currency will be more unfavorable entering 2H24, hence making currency impact on apparel/footwear OEMs' margin/earnings more neutral, which is our base case assumption.

We reduce our EPS forecasts by 2-4% for 2023-25e and introduce 2026e forecasts. We roll over our valuation to 2024e from 4Q23-3Q24e EPS.

We trim our sales assumptions by 1-3% for 2023-25e.

Our gross margin estimates rise slightly in view of Eclat's solid trajectory of gross margin expansion in 2023 and we tweak our margin assumption.

Overall, that leads to a decrease of net profit by 2-4%in 2023-25e.

Our price target is revised to NT\$680 (previously NT\$700) to reflect our earnings cuts and roll forward of our valuation to 2024, from 4Q23-3Q24e. We maintain our target multiple at 26x, vs. the stock's 13-37x range since its re-rating in 2015. This reflects our positive stance on Eclat's quality growth track, supported by its strong R&D capability on top of supply-side consolidation. We believe an optimistic outlook for 2024 with both revenue and margin upside should continue to drive the structural up-cycle. Thus, we see room for re-rating.

Our bull and bear case values decrease to NT\$925 (from NT\$950) and NT\$350 (from NT \$360), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

We reduce our EPS forecasts by 5-11% for 2023-25 and introduce 2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by 0-3% for 2023-25.

Our margin estimates are lowered in view of Makalot's lower utilization rate.

Overall, this has led to a decrease in net profit by 5-11% in 2023-25.

Our price target is revised to NT\$375 (previously NT\$430) as we trim our earnings assumption and roll our valuation forward to 2024, from 4Q23-3Q24 with target multiple revised down to 21x from 23x. We lower our PE multiple because the 4Q23 earnings miss may cap further re-rating potential, as most of the positives appear to be in the price. Our target multiple is only slightly higher than its average PE ratio in the prior upcycle of 20x (after the re-rating in 2H23), and is within the stock's 10-year historical PE range of 9-33x.

Our bull and bear case values are lowered to NT\$590 (from NT\$680) and NT\$190 (from NT\$230), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

We reduce our EPS forecasts by 7-19% for 2023-25 and introduce 2026 forecasts. We roll over our valuation to 2024e from 2H23-1H24e EPS.

We trim our sales assumptions by 2-5% for 2023-25.

Our GPM estimates are slightly reduced by 0.6ppt for 2024 and 0.5ppt for 2025, while our OPM estimates are trimmed by 0.4ppt for 2023 0.5ppt for 2024 and 0.4ppt for 2025.

Our pretax profit estimates are lowered to factor in FX losses for 2023 along with revised non-op assumptions for 2024-2025 in view of FX changes.

Overall, that leads to a decrease in net profit by 7-19% in 2023-25.

Our price target is revised to NT\$175 (from NT\$180) as a result of lowered target multiple of 14.5x from 15x with valuation rolling forward to 2024, from 4Q23-3Q24.

We lowered our market multiple as an uncertain economy outlook may put more pressure on market sentiment toward smaller footwear OEMs. But this reduction is relatively minor as we believe despite the impact of broad-based macro conditions on the footwear OEM segment, Fulgent Sun will be more resilient than peers. We consider our target P/E reasonable vs. our covered Asia apparel/footwear OEMs; it implies a PEG of 0.58 vs. its historical range of 0.21x to 1.17x. Fulgent Sun's historical P/E range has been 5x to 52x (average of 14.2x) since its Oct-12 IPO.

Our bull and bear case values are decreased to NT\$263 (from NT\$275) and NT\$68 (from NT\$70), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

We reduce our EPS forecasts by 3-8% for 2023-25 and introduce 2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by 1-5% for 2023-25.

Our GPM assumptions rise by 0.2ppt for 2024 and 0.3ppt for 2025, while our OPM estimates increase slightly by 0.1-0.2ppt for 2023-2025 to factor in 4Q23 preliminary results.

We also revise our non-operating assumptions to factor in the FX loss in 4Q23 and trim pretax assumptions for 2024-2025 in view of reset FX situation.

Overall, that leads to a decrease of net profit by 3-8% in 2023-25.

Our price target is revised to NT\$135 (previously NT\$140) as we trim our earnings and roll our valuation forward to 2024, from 4Q23-3Q24

Our target multiple is raised to 18.5x from 18x, up just 3% in view of the premium to larger caps in a tough macro. This offset by lukewarm fundamentals, although an uncertain global economic outlook may see market sentiment more positive on leading footwear OEMs. Our 18.5x multiple compares to its historical range (since its re-rating began in 2015) of 13-39x. We consider it reasonable as we project single-digit earnings growth for 2024-26 vs. high-teens growth pre-Covid, in view of the headwinds we expect.

Our bull and bear case values are decreased to NT\$245 (from NT\$255) and NT\$80 (from NT\$83), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

We reduce our EPS forecasts by 13-31% for 2023-25e and introduce 2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by 5-6% for 2023-25.

Our GPM assumptions fall 1.5ppt, 1.7ppt and 1.77 ppt for 2023, 2024, and 2025, respectively, while our OPM estimates decline 3.0ppt , 2.8pptand 2.8ppt for 4Q 2023, 2024, and 2025, respectively, to factor in results .

We also revise our non-operating assumptions to factor in the FX loss in 4Q23 and re-set FX assumptions for 2025-2025.

Overall, that leads to a decrease of net profit by 13-31% in 2023-25.

Our price target, however, rises to NT\$45 (previously NT\$40) as we roll our valuation forward to 2024, from 4Q23-3Q24.

Our target multiple increases to 11x from 10x in view of better recovery momentum vs. the timing seen in August-23, and as market sentiment may be favorable to a stock with low expectations given slightly better market sentiment than seen in the second half of 2023. That said, we think the tough macro and its continuous investment in capex might still weigh on its fundamentals. Our target P/E compares to a median P/E of 14x since 2013; we consider it justified by its earnings prospects (net profit CAGR of 3.5% for 2024- 26e), as well as our view that the tough macro and its continuous investment in capex could weigh on fundamentals.

Our bull and bear case values are decreased to NT\$70 (from NT\$63) and NT\$23 (from NT \$20), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

We reduce our EPS forecasts by 3-11% for 2023-25e and introduce 2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by 1-3% for 2023-25.

We adjust our GPM assumption by 0.1ppt for 2023 to factor in ongoing gross margin improvement, but tweak our assumptions for 2024-25 by 0.5ppt. This leads to a similar magnitude of changes to OPM assumptions.

We also revise our non-operating assumptions to factor in the FX loss in 2H23.

Overall, that leads to a decrease of net profit by 3-11% in 2023-25e.

Our price target is maintained at HK\$100 with unchanged multiple of 23x along with valuation rolling forward to 2024, from 2H23-1H24. Our multiple compares to its historical 11-34x range since re-rating in 2012. We believe sequential business recovery for Shenzhou will drive re-rating, shifting the market's focus to its solid business model that could obtain more market share amid industry consolidation, boosting long-term earnings growth. We think its superior long-term industry position merits a premium to peers.

Our bull and bear case values are unchanged at HK\$155 and HK\$50, respectively.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

We reduce our EPS forecasts by 14-18% for 2023-25 and introduce 2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by 7-8% for 2023-25.

Our GPM estimates are cut by 0.3ppt, 0.5ppt and 0.6ppt for 2023, 2024 and 2025, respectively, to factor in lingering performance of the retail business.

However, we tweak out OPM assumptions by just 0.2ppt for 2023, 0.1ppt for 2024 and 0.0ppt for 2025 in view of stringent opex control at Yue Yuen.

We also revise our non-operating assumptions to factor in the FX loss in 2H23.

Overall, that leads to a decrease of net profit by 14-18% in 2023-25.

Our price target is cut to HK\$8.5 (previously HK\$10) as we trim our earnings and roll our valuation forward to 2024, from 2H23-1H24. Our target multiple is cut to 7.5x from 8.5x as the lingering retail business outlook in China should weigh on stock momentum. Our target P/E is 25% below the lowest point of YY's historical range of 10-18x in 2012-18 (excluding 2H17-1H18 because YY had a significant earnings decline in 2018, making forward P/E unrealistic). We think such a target multiple is fair given our view on lingering uncertainty in the outlook and de-rating of Hong Kong's equity market.

Our bull and bear case values are lowered to HK\$13.1 (from HK\$15.4) and HK\$4.5 (from HK \$5.3), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

We lift our EPS forecasts by 8% for 2023-25 and introduce 2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by -1\~+1% for 2023-25.

Our GPM and OPM estimates are lifted due to improvement of product mix and stringent OPEX control.

Overall, that leads to an increase in net profit by 8% in 2023-25e.

Our price target is lifted to HK\$12 (previously HK\$10) as we raise our earnings estimates and roll our valuation forward to 2024, from 2H23-1H24. Our target multiple remains unchanged at 8x as Stella's performance should be resilient in the current economic environment. We view our multiple as reasonable given the wider discount to our target P/E for Taiwan-listed Feng Tay in view of the difference in market nature between Taiwan and Hong Kong and Feng Tay being a large footwear OEM for Nike.

Our bull and bear case values are increased to HK\$20.3 (from HK\$16.9) and HK\$4.6 (from HK\$3.8), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

We factor in 3Q23 results.

We reduce our EPS forecasts by 0-10% for 2023-25 and introduce 2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by 3-7% sales for 2023-25.

We also revise our non-operating assumptions to factor in the FX loss in 2H23.

Overall, that leads to a decrease of net profit by 0-10% in 2023-25e.

However, despite these reductions, our price target rises to RMB62 (previously RMB55) as we lift our P/E to 18.5x from 16.5x. This reflects our view that Huali will continue to benefit from supply chain consolidation that could lead to a rare market share gain story amid a tough macro. We also roll our valuation forward to 2024, from 2H23- 1H24. We think our multiple is reasonable from a supply chain position and profitability perspective, vs. its historical trading range of 14-45x since its IPO on April 26, 2021.

Our bull and bear case values increase to RMB83 (from RMB74) and RMB32 (from RMB28).

Source: Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

We cut our EPS forecasts by 31-37% for F2024-25 and introduce F2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by 11\~13% for 2023-25e.

Our GPM estimates are cut by 0.8-1.0ppt for F2024-25e as tougher macro conditions weigh on utilization rates. Our OPM forecasts are revised down accordingly.

We tweaked non-op assumptions to factor in higher financing costs.

Overall, that leads to a decrease of net profit by 31-37% in 2023-25e.

Our price target is lowered to HK\$2.2 (previously HK\$4.7) as we trim our earnings and roll our valuation forward to CY2024, from CY2023

Our target multiple is lowered to 6.5x from 11x as interest expense control could weigh on earnings growth, and as the company might not see near-term improvement in its debt level to support any significant cut in the non op expense. Our target multiple is at the low end of its historical PE range of 6.5-12.3x (since its de-rating 2H22), and 27% below the historical average of 9x during the de-rating period. We consider this reasonable considering lingering uncertainty in the domestic business outlook and de-rating in the market. Our bull and bear case values are decreased to HK\$4.07 (from HK\$8.7) and HK \$0.68 (from HK\$2.86), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates

We cut our EPS forecasts by 17-31% for 2023-25 and introduce 2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by 11-15% for 2023-25e.

Our GPM assumptions are cut by 0.5-1.2ppt for 2024-2026e to factor in the lingering macro outlook weighing on utilization rates. Our OPM assumptions are revised down accordingly.

Overall, that leads to a decrease of net profit by 17-31% in 2023-25e.

Our price target is cut to HK\$1.05 (previously HK\$1.45) as we trim our earnings and roll our valuation forward to 2024, from 2H23-1H24. Our target multiple is lowered to 5.5x from 6x due to ongoing transition of products and capacity, creating pressures on revenue and earnings, with worsening fundamentals dragging the earnings outlook despite a slightly better macro sentiment vs. that seen in August 23. Our multiple is at the low end of the company's historical range of 6-18x (2010-YTD). We view a 54% discount to its average P/E multiple of 12x as justifiable due to ongoing transition of products and capacity, pressuring revenue and earnings.

Our bull and bear case values are decreased to HK\$1.38 (from HK\$1.91) and HK\$0.59 (from HK\$0.82), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

## We adjust our EPS forecasts by -1% to +4% for 2023-25e and introduce 2026 forecasts.

We trim our sales assumptions by 0-2% for 2023-25e.

Our GPM assumptions rise 0.1ppt for 2024-2025 in view of better utilization rates on the back of cyclical recovery.

Overall, that leads to net profits being adjusted by -1% to +4% in 2023-25e.

Our price target is lifted to HK\$3.3 (previously HK\$2.4) as we trim our earnings and roll our valuation forward to 2024, from 2H23-1H24

Our target multiple is lifted to 7x (from 5.5x) as we think the cyclical recovery will benefit large apparel OEMs that could resume earnings growth. We find this multiple reasonable at a discount to Taiwan-listed Makalot's target P/E in view of Makalot's better execution track record, as well as the difference in market nature between Taiwan and Hong Kong.

Our bull and bear case values are increased to HK\$5.5 (from HK\$4.0) and HK\$1.35 (from HK\$0.98), in line with the magnitude of change in our base-case value.

## Risk Reward - Eclat Tex<sub>Risk Reward – Eclat Textile (1476.TW)</sub>

Intact Upcoming Earnings Upcycle; Stay OW

## NT\$680.00PRICE TARGET

Base case, P/E. We apply a 26x target multiple to our 2024e estimate vs. the stock's 13-37x range since its re-rating in 2015. This re ects our positive stance on Eclat's quality growth track, supported by its strong R&D capability on top of supply-side consolidation. We believe an optimistic outlook for 2024 with both revenue and margin upside should continue to drive the structural up-cycle. Thus, we see room for re-rating.

Source: Re finitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We appreciate Eclat's prudent approach in managing customer relationships, ongoing product innovation, and seeking quality over quantity.

▪ We like its position as a major bene ciary of resilient demand for functional wear thanks to rising awareness of health and a major customer's international expansion. ▪ The stock is trading near 26x 2024e P/E, which we view as attractive, implying a PEG < 1.

## BULL CASE

## 29x 2024e EPS

## NT\$925.00 BASE CASE

Faster adoption of knitted fabrics in sportswear; robust growth from high-end orders and new order wins: Eclat gains signi cant order growth from current customers and expands its customer base aggressively. 2024 gross margin is above 35.0%.

## 26x 2024e EPS

## NT\$680.00 BEAR CASE

Functional fabrics remain the trend for sportswear, while garment growth is good thanks to a higher proportion of internal sourcing: Eclat remains the leading fabric provider in Taiwan, and expands its base with some new customers. 2024 gross margin is 30.0% and operating margin is 21.5%.

## NT\$350.00

## 22x 2024e EPS

Eclat faces more competition from Chinese peers; end demand in the US and EU turns sluggish: Chinese peers achieve Eclat's level of quality, so the company has to cut ASP to maintain customer orders. 2024 gross margin falls below 20%.

Watch your step

## NT\$135.00PRICE TARGET

Base case, derived via comparative P/E analysis. We apply 18.5x P/E to our 2024e EPS estimate. Feng Tay's historical P/E range (since its re-rating began in 2015) is 13-39x. We think our target P/E multiple is reasonable as we project single-digit earnings growth for 2024-26 vs. high-teens growth pre-Covid, in view of the headwinds we expect.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

We see three key headwinds for Feng Tay: ▪ We believe rising divergence in the
athleisure footwear category could result in limited growth opportunities for the
company.
▪ It faces increasing competition in
Indonesian and Indian manufacturing, which could be a long-term potential headwind. ▪ We think a diversi fied customer base may cap further margin upside.
Based on our revised estimates, we view valuation as stretched vs. our reduced target P/E of 18.5x.
We expect de-rating to ensue as these
headwinds manifest themselves.

![](images/c6f79e53dfe9b2412b93b1f281613be80abc35b60822e942808e4f923e266119.jpg)

# Risk Reward - Fulgent Sun International H<sub>Risk Reward – Fulgent Sun International Holding Co Ltd (9802.TW)</sub>

(9802.TW)A Step Ahead of Footwear Megatrends

## NT\$175.00PRICE TARGET

Our price target is based on 14.5x our 2024e EPS, which we consider reasonable vs. our covered Asia apparel/footwear OEMs. Our target P/E implies a PEG of 0.58 vs. its historical range of 0.21x to 1.17x – reasonable, in our view. Fulgent Sun's historical P/E range has been 5x to 52x (average of 14.2x) since its Oct-12 IPO.

## RISK REWARD CHART

![](images/27a4ebf2cfbdff844299ad061090bce69219e36b7e27428e623fd75c2c2cb95c.jpg)
Key: Historical Stock Performance Current Stock Price Price Target
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Fulgent Sun continues to win new customers and bene t from outdoor footwear growth momentum, bolstered by its scale advantage, execution and operational ef <sup>fi</sup>ciency. We expect this will drive superior long-term margins. ▪ The company is positioned to bene t from global supply-chain rebalancing, diversi fication by customers and consumer adoption of shoes worn across different occasions, which bodes well for structural top-line growth.

▪ We rate the shares OW, driven by underappreciated value in its diversi ed brand portfolio and manufacturing expertise, positioning it to meet rising global demand.

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## 18.5x 2024e EPS

NT\$263.00

Global restocking demand for footwear is faster than expected: Fulgent Sun continues to gain share within its current customer portfolios. With its leadership position in functional footwear and continuous development of complex products, with solid operational execution, it delivers a better margin pro le. Earnings grow at a >35% CAGR, 2024-2026, and the operating margin expands to 18.8% in 2024.

## 14.5x 2024e EPS

## NT\$175.00

Ongoing market share gains within existing clients with new client additions; expanded operating margins on good opex control: Fulgent Sun is able to expand gross and operating pro t margins thanks to its innovation capability and operating fief ciency amid top-line growth recovery. Sales to increase at a 14% CAGR, 2024-26, with operating margin expanding to 14.8% in 2024.

## BEAR CASE

## 7.5x 2024e EPS

NT\$68.00

Industry destocking persists in 2024 due to unfavorable macro leading to shipment declines and softer ASP requests from customers, mismatching raw material price declines: A greater top-line decline results in operating deleverage despite good opex control. Overall, sales decline at a 3% CAGR, 2024-2026, and the operating margin declines to 9.0% in 2024.

Lingering Retail Business to Cap Upcycle; Stay EW

## HK\$8.50PRICE TARGET

Base case, derived via comparative P/E analysis. We apply a target P/E of 7.5x to our 2024e EPS estimate, which is 25% below the lowest point of YY's historical range of 10-18x in 2012- 2018 (excluding 2H17-1H18 because YY had a signi <sup>fi</sup>cant earnings decline in 2018, making forward P/E unrealistic). We think such a target multiple is fair given our view on lingering uncertainty in the outlook and de-rating of Hong Kong's equity market.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ We nd current valuation for the stock insuf ciently attractive considering the
uncertain timing of domestic consumption recovery and limited near-term catalysts because of low visibility.
▪ In the manufacturing business, uncertain macro conditions and customers' inventory digestion cycle make order visibility low and shipments volatile, lowering the capacity utilization rate.
▪ We are cautious on the outlook for retail (Pou Sheng) into 2024 in view of uncertain domestic consumption trends in China.

## Risk Reward - Shenzhou International G<sub>Risk Reward – Shenzhou International Group Holdings (2313.HK)</sub>

(2313.HK)Competitive Edges to Strengthen Fundamentals; Stay OW

## HK\$100.00PRICE TARGET

23x base-case 2024e EPS, vs. its historical 11-34x range since rerating in 2012. We believe sequential business recovery for Shenzhou will drive re-rating, shifting the market's focus to its solid business model that could obtain more market share amid industry consolidation, boosting long-term earnings growth. We think its superior long-term industry position merits a premium to peers.

## HK\$100.00

## 23x base-case 2024 EPS (HKD)

End market recovery to drive ASP and shipment improvement, with continuous improvement on utilization to boost margins: We expect: 1) steady capacity increases, ASP enhancement as a result of better product mix and rising raw material prices drive sales CAGR of 15% in 2024- 2026e; and 2) operating margin rises to 19.5% in 2024 (from 2023 of 16.2%), spurred by operating leverage and higher gross margin, driving 23% earnings growth in 2024.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Stella is a niche footwear OEM focusing on growth from Nike and luxury brands

consolidation efforts.

growth with margin expansion providing downside support.
▪ Our price target of HK\$12 is based on 8x 2024e P/E. The current P/E is still below that, and we think the market is overlooking Stella's diversi ed customer portfolio to further support its pro ts performance.

conditions, and margins.

## Risk Reward - Regina Miracle Internation<sub>Risk Reward – Regina Miracle International (Holdings) (2199.HK)</sub>

(2199.HK)Earnings Growth Outlook Limited by Financing Costs; EW

## HK\$2.20PRICE TARGET

Our price target is our base-case scenario value. It is derived from a target P/E multiple of 6.5x 2024e EPS. Our target multiple is at the low end of its historical P/E range of 6.5- 12.3x (since its de-rating 2H22), and 27% below the historical average of 9x during the derating period. We consider this reasonable considering lingering uncertainty in the domestic business outlook and de-rating in the market.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ We like the company's position toward continuous R&D development, and resumed strength of key customers should drive ongoing margin expansion and its position in the apparel and footwear OEMs segment. ▪ However, its short- to medium-term <sup>fi</sup>nancing cost controls and debt levels might limit earnings growth upside potential.

## BULL CASE

HK\$4.07

## 9.5x bull-case 2024e EPS

Stronger than expected earnings growth: 1) Stronger sales growth, helped by greater product extension into footwear and activewear. 2) Gross margin trends rise more as a result of quicker mix shift toward higher-margin categories. 3) Operating margin improves toward 15% in F2024e.

## BASE CASE

## 6.5x base-case 2024e EPS

## HK\$2.20

Sustained earnings growth outlook: 1) Overall sales CAGR at 5%, F2024-26e. 2) Gross margin gradually improves, driven by product mix and raw material cost savings in the near term and Vietnam cost savings in the longer term. 3) Operating margin improves to 9.6% in F2024e.

## BEAR CASE

HK\$0.68

## 3.5x bear-case 2024e EPS

Weaker than expected earnings growth outlook: 1) Weaker sales growth on endretail-demand slowdown. 2) Gross margin declines due to rising labor costs and failure to pass through cost pressure. 3) Operating margin contracts to less than 6% in F2024e.

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index
